<result>Here is the complete technical breakdown of Rust's projectile validation system, silent aim, and magic bullet mechanics, sourced from decompiled server code, Facepunch devblogs, and community research.

---

## 1. Does Rust's server validate projectile trajectories?

**Yes, extensively.** Rust uses a client-authoritative projectile model with layered server-side verification. The client simulates projectiles locally, detects hits, and reports them to the server via RPC. The server then replays / validates the projectile against its own simplified physics model before accepting the hit.

From decompiled `BasePlayer.cs` (unet-dev/Decompiled-Assemblies), the server stores every fired projectile in a `Dictionary&lt;int, FiredProjectile&gt; firedProjectiles` keyed by projectileID. The `FiredProjectile` struct records:

```
initialPosition, initialVelocity   -- recorded at fire time, never updated
position, velocity                 -- updated as the server tracks projectile movement
firedTime, travelTime, partialTime -- timing data
integrity                          -- penetration remaining (starts at 1.0)
protection                         -- the antihack level active at fire time
projectilePrefab                   -- has gravityModifier, drag, initialDistance
```

At protection level &gt;= 4, the server runs `SimulateProjectile()` which performs a full physics replay:

```csharp
void SimulateProjectile(ref Vector3 position, ref Vector3 velocity, 
    ref float partialTime, float travelTime, float deltaTime, 
    Vector3 gravity, float drag)
{
    // handles partial time from previous frame
    // then iterates: position += velocity * dt; velocity += gravity * dt; velocity -= velocity * drag * dt
    // stores remainder in partialTime
}
```

The server compares where it thinks the projectile should be vs. where the client claims the hit occurred, checking both horizontal and vertical deviation against `ConVar.AntiHack.projectile_trajectory_horizontal` and `projectile_trajectory_vertical`.

Sources:
- [Decompiled BasePlayer.cs](https://github.com/unet-dev/Decompiled-Assemblies/blob/master/Assembly-CSharp/BasePlayer.cs) (lines 2826-3025, 3137-3220, 4589-4611)
- [Devblog 123](https://rust.facepunch.com/news/devblog-123)

---

## 2. How does ConVar.AntiHack work for projectile validation?

The `antihack.projectile_protection` ConVar controls the depth of validation. Each level is cumulative:

| Level | Checks Added |
|-------|-------------|
| 0 | Disabled -- no projectile validation |
| 1 | **Speed check**: `distance(initialPosition, hitPositionWorld) &gt; initialDistance + desyncTime * initialVelocity.magnitude` -- rejects "traveled too fast" |
| 2 | **Entity distance**: checks if the hit entity is physically reachable given its max velocity, bounds padding, and desync allowance |
| 3 | **Line of sight**: `GamePhysics.LineOfSight()` from last known projectile position to hit point. Also checks LOS from hit point to target's eyes/center. Applied on updates and ricochets too |
| 4 | **Trajectory replay**: server runs `SimulateProjectile()` to replay projectile physics, then checks horizontal and vertical deviation between simulated position and reported hit/update position |
| 5 | **Velocity override**: on updates and ricochets, the server overwrites the client-reported velocity with the server-simulated velocity (`playerProjectileUpdate.curVelocity = vector33`). Also adds collider checks on ricochets via `GamePhysics.CheckSphere()` |
| 6 | Default on official servers -- includes all of the above plus tickhistory |

Key ConVars and their defaults:

| ConVar | Default | Purpose |
|--------|---------|---------|
| `antihack.projectile_protection` | 6 | Protection level (0-6) |
| `antihack.projectile_penalty` | 0 | Violation points per infraction |
| `antihack.projectile_forgiveness` | 0.5 | Speed tolerance multiplier (50% extra = `1.0 + 0.5 = 1.5x`) |
| `antihack.projectile_padding` | 5 | Hit distance padding in meters |
| `antihack.projectile_serverframes` | 2 | Server frame delay allowance |
| `antihack.projectile_clientframes` | 2 | Client frame delay allowance |
| `antihack.projectile_tickets` | 10 | LOS violations before flagging |
| `antihack.projectile_trajectory_horizontal` | (threshold) | Max allowed horizontal deviation in meters |
| `antihack.projectile_trajectory_vertical` | (threshold) | Max allowed vertical deviation in meters |

**Desync time budget calculation** (from the decompiled code):
```
desyncTime = (player.desyncTime + timeSinceFired + clientFrames/60 + serverFrames*maxDeltaTime) * forgiveness
```

This budget is used in all distance/speed calculations to account for network latency and prevent false positives.

**Special case**: if the player `HasParent()` or `isMounted`, protection is capped at level 3 (no trajectory replay). This was later improved with the `antihack.parenthistory` ConVar to track parent entity positions.

Sources:
- [RustDS Commands Gist](https://gist.github.com/sarimoko/110e9dcb65f6da9dbc7a9849e919f761)
- [AntiHack GitBook](https://rust-console-edition.gitbook.io/rust-console-edition-community-servers/commands/moderation/antihack)
- [RustCommands GitHub](https://github.com/bitfabrikken/RustCommands)
- [Decompiled BasePlayer.cs](https://github.com/unet-dev/Decompiled-Assemblies/blob/master/Assembly-CSharp/BasePlayer.cs)

---

## 3. Can you manipulate initialPosition or initialVelocity to hit a different target?

**Partially, but the server validates both at fire time.**

When the client fires via the `CLProject` RPC, it sends a `ProjectileShoot` message containing per-projectile `startPos` and `startVel`. The server processes this in `BaseProjectile.CLProject()`:

```csharp
basePlayer.NoteFiredProjectile(projectile.projectileID, projectile.startPos, 
    projectile.startVel, this, itemDefinition, null);
```

Inside `NoteFiredProjectile()`, the server immediately:

1. **Validates NaN/Infinity**: rejects if `startPos.IsNaNOrInfinity() || startVel.IsNaNOrInfinity()`
2. **Speed-checks initialVelocity** (protection &gt;= 1): `startVel.magnitude` must not exceed `maxVelocity * forgiveness` where maxVelocity comes from `ItemModProjectile.GetMaxVelocity() * weapon.GetProjectileVelocityScale()`
3. **Stores both as immutable references**: `firedProjectile.initialPosition = startPos` and `firedProjectile.initialVelocity = startVel`

The `initialPosition` and `initialVelocity` are never updated after storage -- they are used later in the speed check (protection &gt;= 1) which computes:
```
maxDistance = projectilePrefab.initialDistance + desyncTime * initialVelocity.magnitude
actualDistance = Vector3.Distance(initialPosition, hitPositionWorld)
```

So you can spoof `startPos` / `startVel` in the fire RPC to some extent, but:
- The velocity magnitude must stay within weapon bounds
- The distance between `initialPosition` and the eventual hit point must be physically plausible given the velocity and time elapsed
- At protection &gt;= 4, the server replays the trajectory from the tracked position using stored physics parameters and checks deviation

The mutable fields `position` and `velocity` are updated via `OnProjectileUpdate` RPCs, but at protection &gt;= 5, the server overwrites the client's reported velocity with its own simulated velocity.

Sources:
- [Decompiled BaseProjectile.cs](https://github.com/unet-dev/Decompiled-Assemblies/blob/master/Assembly-CSharp/BaseProjectile.cs)
- [Decompiled BasePlayer.cs](https://github.com/unet-dev/Decompiled-Assemblies/blob/master/Assembly-CSharp/BasePlayer.cs) (lines 2408-2472)

---

## 4. What does the server actually check when a projectile hit is reported?

The `OnProjectileAttack` RPC handler (line 2826) processes `PlayerProjectileAttack` messages. Here is the full validation pipeline in order:

**Pre-validation (always runs):**
1. **NaN/Infinity check** -- `hitInfo.IsNaNOrInfinity()`, `travelTime` NaN/Inf
2. **ProjectileID lookup** -- must exist in `firedProjectiles` dictionary, logs `projectile_invalid` if missing
3. **Integrity check** -- `firedProjectile.integrity &gt; 0`, logs `projectile_integrity` if zero (projectile already fully penetrated/stopped)
4. **Lifetime check** -- `firedTime &lt; realtimeSinceStartup - 8f` means projectile expired (8 second max lifetime), logs `projectile_lifetime`

**Protection level checks (cumulative):**

5. **Level &gt;= 1 -- Speed**: `distance(initialPosition, hitPositionWorld) &gt; initialDistance + desyncBudget * initialVelocity.magnitude` --&gt; logs `projectile_speed`

6. **Level &gt;= 2 -- Entity distance**: For the hit entity, computes `maxReach = entity.BoundsPadding() + desyncBudget * (entity.MaxVelocity() + entity.GetParentVelocity().magnitude)`. If `entity.Distance(hitPositionWorld) &gt; maxReach` --&gt; logs `projectile_distance`

7. **Level &gt;= 3 -- Line of sight (two checks)**:
   - LOS from last projectile position to hitPointStart to hitPositionWorld (4-point check via `GamePhysics.LineOfSight`) --&gt; logs `projectile_los`
   - If target is a player: LOS from hitPositionWorld to target's `eyes.position` and `CenterPoint()` -- both must fail to flag --&gt; logs `projectile_los`

8. **Level &gt;= 4 -- Trajectory replay**: `SimulateProjectile()` from last known position/velocity using stored gravity and drag, then checks:
   - `(hitPositionWorld - simulatedPosition).Magnitude2D() &gt; projectile_trajectory_horizontal` --&gt; logs `horizontal_trajectory`
   - `Abs((hitPositionWorld - simulatedPosition).y) &gt; projectile_trajectory_vertical` --&gt; logs `vertical_trajectory`

If ANY check fails, `AntiHack.AddViolation(player, ProjectileHack, projectile_penalty)` is called and the hit is rejected.

**Post-validation (hit accepted):**
- Damage calculated via `ProjectilePrefab.CalculateDamage(hitInfo, modifier, integrity)`
- Integrity reduced by `PenetrationResistance / penetrationPower` (or set to 0 if no penetration)
- FiredProjectile state updated: `position = hitPositionWorld`, `velocity = hitVelocity`, `travelTime` updated

Sources:
- [Decompiled BasePlayer.cs](https://github.com/unet-dev/Decompiled-Assemblies/blob/master/Assembly-CSharp/BasePlayer.cs) (lines 2826-3025)
- [Pastebin server code](https://pastebin.com/3BZGhBBv)

---

## 5. How does magic bullet work -- manipulating projectile data vs view angles?

Based on the architecture, there are two distinct approaches:

**Silent Aim (client-side hit spoofing):**
The client simulates projectiles locally. When a projectile updates (`DoMovement`), the cheat can manipulate the hit detection to report a hit on a nearby enemy even though the projectile trajectory was aimed elsewhere. The client sends a `PlayerProjectileAttack` with:
- `hitPositionWorld` adjusted to the target's position
- `hitDistance` and `hitVelocity` calculated to be consistent
- `travelTime` matching expected flight time
The view angles (camera) never change -- the crosshair stays where the player is looking, but the hit report goes to the server claiming the bullet hit someone else.

**Magic Bullet (deeper projectile manipulation):**
The cheat intercepts the fire RPC and/or the projectile simulation to:
1. **Modify `startPos` / `startVel`** in the `ProjectileShoot` message to aim the projectile at the target from the server's perspective, while the client renders it going in the original direction
2. **Manipulate `OnProjectileUpdate` RPCs** to feed the server a trajectory that converges on the target
3. **Adjust `hitPositionWorld`, `hitDistance`, `hitVelocity`, `travelTime`** in the attack RPC to be physically consistent with the spoofed trajectory

The key difference: silent aim keeps the server's recorded trajectory as-is and just spoofs the final hit report, while magic bullet tries to make the entire trajectory plausible to the server by manipulating the fire + update + hit messages together. Magic bullet is more resilient against trajectory replay (protection &gt;= 4) but requires more precise physics calculations to stay within tolerance.

From the GitHub cheat source analysis (hybrid-analysis sample), the hooks are:
- `OnProjectileAttack` -- intercept hit reports
- `OnProjectileUpdate` -- intercept position updates
- `OnProjectileRicochet` -- intercept ricochet events
- `NoteFiredProjectile` -- intercept fire registration

Sources:
- [Hybrid Analysis sample](https://hybrid-analysis.com/sample/e03277aabb29a87fedcf91f517800dc3397122b90540d5bc88b766b2e16227dd/5f90bf92a6a25962c96a7bb8)
- [Cheat source structure](https://github.com/coomed/rust-internal-source/blob/main/projectile.hpp)
- [GitHub neko-rust-internal](https://github.com/exliation/neko-rust-internal)

---

## 6. What server-side checks catch magic bullet / silent aim?

**Checks that catch naive silent aim:**
- **Trajectory replay (level 4+)**: If the bullet was clearly aimed at position A but the hit claims position B, `SimulateProjectile()` will show the expected position is nowhere near the reported hit. The horizontal/vertical deviation thresholds catch this.
- **Speed check (level 1+)**: If the hit position is farther than `initialDistance + desyncBudget * velocity` from the fire position, it is flagged as `projectile_speed`.
- **LOS check (level 3+)**: The 4-point line-of-sight check from the projectile's last known position through the hit path will fail if the bullet would have had to pass through geometry to reach the target.

**Checks that catch naive magic bullet:**
- **Initial velocity validation**: `NoteFiredProjectile` rejects fire RPCs where `startVel.magnitude &gt; maxVelocity * forgiveness`.
- **Entity distance (level 2+)**: Validates the target entity is close enough to where the projectile could physically reach.
- **Trajectory replay on updates (level 4+)**: `OnProjectileUpdate` also runs `SimulateProjectile()` and checks deviation -- so feeding fake intermediate positions that deviate too much from the physics model will be caught.
- **Velocity override (level 5+)**: The server replaces the client's reported velocity with its own simulated velocity on updates: `playerProjectileUpdate.curVelocity = vector33`. This means the cheat cannot report arbitrary velocities to steer the server's tracking.
- **Ricochet collider check (level 5+)**: `GamePhysics.CheckSphere()` at ricochet points verifies an actual surface exists there.

**What the desync budget enables (the attacker's window):**
The tolerance formula `(desyncTime + timeSinceFired + clientFrames/60 + serverFrames*maxDeltaTime) * 1.5` creates a margin of several meters depending on ping. At 100ms ping, the speed check allows roughly `velocityMagnitude * ~0.4s * 1.5 = significant meters` of deviation. This is the window that sophisticated cheats exploit -- they keep manipulations within the desync tolerance.

**What the server does NOT check (as of this decompiled version):**
- No server-side aim direction validation -- the server does not verify that the player's view angles pointed anywhere near where they fired
- No shot consistency analysis across multiple hits (pattern detection is separate from the per-projectile antihack)
- The `projectile_penalty` default is 0, meaning violations are logged but may not result in immediate action unless configured

**Third-party detection (Guardian plugin):**
Server admins can install plugins like Guardian (uMod) that add additional layers including trajectory percentage analysis, comparing reported vs actual projectile distance as a forgery likelihood metric.

Sources:
- [Decompiled BasePlayer.cs](https://github.com/unet-dev/Decompiled-Assemblies/blob/master/Assembly-CSharp/BasePlayer.cs)
- [Guardian plugin](https://umod.org/plugins/guardian)
- [Steam discussion on projectile invalid](https://steamcommunity.com/app/252490/discussions/0/599643430010779293/)
- [Built Different devblog](https://rust.facepunch.com/news/built-different)
- [Devblog 75 - projectile system](https://rust.facepunch.com/news/devblog-75)
- [Devblog 123 - server verification improvements](https://rust.facepunch.com/news/devblog-123)
- [Facepunch commit on antihack convars](https://commits.facepunch.com/248512)
- [Valve wiki - Rust dedicated server](https://developer.valvesoftware.com/wiki/Rust_Dedicated_Server)

- [OwnedCore cheat listing](https://www.ownedcore.com/forums/mmo-trading-market/fps-buy-sell-trade/1040020-rust-silent-aim-esp-customprojectile-no-spread-ore-base-local-player-spiderman-ud.html)
- [AntiHack GitBook docs](https://rust-console-edition.gitbook.io/rust-console-edition-community-servers/commands/moderation/antihack)</result>

## Research Report: Apex Legends Spectator Counter, Ring Prediction, and Miscellaneous Exploits

### 1. SPECTATOR COUNTER

**Status: Still works (as of 2026)**

Respawn removed the official spectator eye icon from the HUD years ago because dead teammates could relay enemy positions to alive squad members. However, the underlying data still exists in game memory and cheats actively read it.

**Technical implementation -- two known approaches:**

**Approach A (Observer List method, from apex_dma_kvm_pub):**
The `Entity::Observing()` function works as follows:
1. Read the spectator list base address from `OFFSET_OBSERVER_LIST` (value: `0x02022b50 + 0x20C8` in the dump examined)
2. Read a player data index from the entity pointer at `ptr + 0x38`
3. Index into the spectator list array: `SpectatorList + PlayerData * 8 + 0x964`
4. Resolve the spectator's entity address via the entity list: `OFFSET_ENTITYLIST + ((SpecIndex &amp; 0xFFFF) &lt;&lt; 5)`
5. Compare the resolved address against the local player pointer -- if equal, that entity is spectating you

Key offsets found:
- `OFFSET_OBSERVER_MODE`: `0x34a4`
- `OFFSET_OBSERVING_TARGET`: `0x34b0`
- `OFFSET_OBSERVER_LIST`: `0x02022b50 + 0x20C8`

**Approach B (Player iteration method, from apexdream):**
The `observers.rs` implementation:
1. Iterates all players, builds a bitset of which teams have living members
2. Classifies dead players as spectators based on observer mode
3. If local player's `observer_mode == OBS_MODE_FIXED`, all dead players are spectators
4. If in free camera mode, dead players from fully-eliminated teams are shown as spectators
5. Compares `server_angles[0..1]` (pitch/yaw) to detect which player a spectator is actively watching -- marked with "!!" prefix when angles match

The `PlayerEntity` struct reads observer state from two consecutive u32 values at `player_observer_state` offset:
- `observer_mode` (i32) at offset + 0
- `observer_target` (EHandle) at offset + 4

From the ReactiioN1337 offset gist (DT_LocalPlayerExclusive):
- `m_iObserverMode = 0x34f4`
- `m_hObserverTarget = 0x3500`
- `m_observerModeStaticPosition = 0x3510`
- `m_observerModeStaticAngles = 0x351c`
- `m_observerModeStaticFOVOverride = 0x3528`

Observer mode constants (from apexdream sdk.rs):
- `OBS_MODE_NONE`
- `OBS_MODE_DEATHCAM`
- `OBS_MODE_FIXED`
- `OBS_MODE_IN_EYE`
- `OBS_MODE_CHASE`
- `OBS_MODE_ROAMING`

**Reliability:** High. The spectator data is inherent to the Source engine networking model -- the client must know who is observing to properly replicate game state. Cheats use this to auto-disable aim assistance and ESP when spectated, making manual ban review harder.

---

### 2. RING / ZONE PREDICTION

**Status: Current ring data is fully readable. Future ring prediction has nuances.**

**Memory structures (from apexdream world.rs and netvar dumps):**

The ring data lives on the World Entity under a `DeathField` structure. From the DT_World netvar table:

| Field | Offset (netvar dump) | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `m_deathFieldIsActive` | `0xAA0` | Boolean -- is the ring active |
| `m_deathFieldOrigin` | `0xAE0` | Vec3 -- ring center position (x,y,z) |
| `m_deathFieldRadiusStart` | `0xDE0` | Float -- starting radius |
| `m_deathFieldRadiusEnd` | `0xEE0` | Float -- ending radius |
| `m_deathFieldTimeStart` | `0xFE0` | Float -- shrink start time |
| `m_deathFieldTimeEnd` | `0x10E0` | Float -- shrink end time |

**These are arrays.** The 0x40-byte stride between array elements (visible in the apexdream code) and the large gaps between offsets (0xAE0 to 0xDE0 = 0x300 gap for origin, 0x100 gaps between radius/time fields) strongly indicate these are arrays indexed by round number. The structure stores data for all rounds simultaneously.

From apexdream's `world.rs`, the memory read pattern is:
- `base = world_death_field offset`
- `is_active`: base + 0
- `origin[3]`: base + 0x40, +0x44, +0x48
- `radius_start`: base + 0x40 + 0x300
- `radius_end`: base + 0x40 + 0x400
- `time_start`: base + 0x40 + 0x500
- `time_end`: base + 0x40 + 0x600

The `radius()` method interpolates between `radius_start` and `radius_end` using `(curtime - time_start) / (time_end - time_start)` clamped to [0,1], giving the current ring radius at any point during the shrink.

The `distance()` method computes how far a player is from the ring edge (positive = outside, negative = inside).

**Future zone prediction:**
Because these are arrays indexed by round, a cheat can potentially read ahead to see future ring centers and radii before they are revealed in-game (normally only accessible via Survey Beacon scan by Recon legends). The critical question is whether the server populates future array indices before each round begins. Based on the array structure, the server likely sends all ring data at match start or incrementally as rounds progress. If pre-populated, full future ring prediction is trivially readable.

The apexdream implementation only uses ring data for a **damage timing indicator** (showing when the next tick hits and whether you have time to heal), not for full future ring position display. But the data structure supports it.

---

### 3. INSTANT HEAL / INSTANT REVIVE

**Status: Mostly server-validated. True "instant heal" is not a simple memory write.**

Apex Legends runs on the Titanfall engine branch (heavily modified Source engine from the Portal 2 branch). Key architecture facts:

- The server validates damage and health state. You cannot simply write a health value to memory and have the server accept it.
- Healing items have server-tracked use times. The server knows when you started using a medkit and when it should complete.
- Reports of "instant heal" cheats on EA forums describe enemies appearing to full-heal mid-fight with no animation. This is more likely explained by desync, animation cancel bugs, or Conduit's abilities than by a client-side memory write.
- A known bug (not a cheat) allowed **instant revives** due to height differences between players during revive -- the animation would cancel and complete instantly. This was a geometry/animation bug, not a memory exploit.

Relevant offsets for health/shield monitoring (read-only for cheats):
- `m_iHealth`: `0x043c` (also `0x0328` in another dump)
- `m_iMaxHealth`: `0x0578`
- `m_shieldHealth`: `0x0170` (also `0x01a0`)
- `m_shieldHealthMax`: `0x0174`
- `m_bleedoutState`: `0x2750` / `0x2760`
- `m_bleedoutStartTime`: `0x2754`
- `m_reviveTarget`: `0x4818`
- `m_dragReviveState`: `0x4810`
- `m_dragReviveOutroStartTime`: `0x4814`

**Bottom line:** Healing and reviving are server-authoritative. Cheats can read these values for ESP (showing enemy health/shield) but cannot write them to instantly heal.

---

### 4. SPEED HACK

**Status: Partially works but actively detected.**

- Respawn confirmed they deployed detection for a "reverse speed hack" that let cheaters freeze/suspend themselves mid-air.
- Movement in Apex has some client authority (Source engine heritage), which is why speed hacks are possible at all, but Respawn has added server-side behavioral checks.
- Speed hacks that are too aggressive get flagged by server-side velocity validation.
- As of April 2026, anti-cheat updates have targeted these. ~1,000 DMA hardware bans were issued specifically. Infection rate dropped to 4.1% from 5.8% in late 2025.

---

### 5. OTHER FEATURES THAT STILL WORK (from analyzed cheat codebases)

**Confirmed working features from apexdream and apex_dma_kvm_pub:**

- **Player ESP**: Position, health, shield, team, name -- all readable from entity list
- **Item/Loot ESP**: Item positions and types readable
- **Glow/Highlight**: Can be toggled via `OFFSET_GLOW_ENABLE` (`0x28C`) -- writes to entity highlight state
- **Aimbot with prediction**: Bullet speed (`OFFSET_BULLET_SPEED`), bullet gravity (`OFFSET_BULLET_SCALE`), zoom FOV (`OFFSET_ZOOM_FOV`) all readable for projectile prediction
- **Recoil compensation**: `OFFSET_AIMPUNCH` (`0x2438`) readable for RCS
- **Radar/minimap overlay**: Entity positions + team data
- **Spectator warning**: As described above
- **Ring damage timing**: As described above
- **View angle reading**: `m_iObserverMode`, camera position, view angles all readable
- **Third person toggle**: `OFFSET_THIRDPERSON` (`0x01e3b190 + 0x6c`)

**What is patched / detected:**
- Aggressive speed hacks (server-side velocity checks)
- DMA hardware cheats are now being detected (~1000 bans in April 2026 wave)
- Server-side aimbot detection flags unnatural acceleration patterns (bypassed by increasing smoothness/humanization)
- The game binary on disk is encrypted; must be dumped from running process memory

---

### 6. KEY TECHNICAL NOTES

- **Engine**: Titanfall engine branch (modified Source/Portal 2 branch) with D3D12 renderer
- **Binary**: `r5apex.exe` is encrypted on disk, must be dumped from memory for offset analysis
- **Offset volatility**: Offsets change with every game update; tools like apexdumper and Apex-Legends-Offset-Dumper automate re-extraction
- **Anti-cheat**: EAC (Easy Anti-Cheat) for driver/file scanning + Respawn's custom server-side behavioral analysis
- **Networking model**: Modified Source engine netvar replication. The client receives entity data including observer state, positions, health, etc. This is fundamentally why read-based cheats (ESP, spectator counter) are so hard to prevent -- the client needs the data to render the game.</result>

## Unturned RPC System and ServerInvocable -- Complete Technical Findings

### 1. Architecture Overview

Unturned uses Remote Procedure Calls (RPCs) for **all gameplay netcode**. Even singleplayer is essentially a one-player server. When a player presses "pick up item [F]", the client asks the server to call a `PickUpItem` method; the server responds by telling the client to call `AddItemToInventory`. The RPC system has gone through multiple rewrites (Network Packing Rewrite, Network Messaging Rewrite, Network RPC Rewrite, NetTransport Rewrite).

---

### 2. The `SteamCall` Attribute (the "ServerInvocable" mechanism)

The `SteamCall` attribute is the core decorator that marks a method as an RPC endpoint. It lives in `SDG.Unturned` namespace. Full definition from datamined source:

```csharp
[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Method)]
public class SteamCall : Attribute
{
    public ESteamCallValidation validation;
    public int ratelimitHz = -1;           // Max calls/sec/player
    public float ratelimitSeconds = -1f;   // Min seconds between calls (derived from ratelimitHz)
    public int rateLimitIndex = -1;        // Index into per-connection rate limiting array
    public string legacyName;              // Backwards compat for old invoke-by-name (plugins)
    public ENetInvocationDeferMode deferMode;

    public SteamCall(ESteamCallValidation validation) {
        this.validation = validation;
    }
}
```

Source: `Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/SteamCall.cs` in the [Unturned-Datamining repo](https://github.com/Unturned-Datamining/Unturned-Datamining/blob/linux-client-preview/Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/SteamCall.cs)

---

### 3. `ESteamCallValidation` Enum

This enum controls **who is allowed to invoke** a given RPC:

```csharp
public enum ESteamCallValidation
{
    NONE,              // No validation
    ONLY_FROM_SERVER,  // Only the server can invoke this on clients
    SERVERSIDE,        // Can only be invoked if we ARE the server
    ONLY_FROM_OWNER    // Only the player who owns this object can invoke it
}
```

Source: `Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/ESteamCallValidation.cs` in the [Unturned-Datamining repo](https://github.com/Unturned-Datamining/Unturned-Datamining/blob/linux-client-preview/Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/ESteamCallValidation.cs)

- `ONLY_FROM_SERVER` -- used on **client receive** methods (server tells client something). Example: `ReceiveDeath`, `ReceiveDead`, `ReceiveRevive`, `ReceiveEquip`.
- `ONLY_FROM_OWNER` -- used on **server receive** methods (client sends request to server, only accepted from the object's owning player). Example: `ReceiveToggleVisionRequest`, `ReceiveInputs`, `ReceiveEquipRequest`.
- `SERVERSIDE` -- must be running as server to execute.
- `NONE` -- no validation (rare/legacy).

---

### 4. RPC Method Types (Send/Receive Pattern)

The system uses **four main handle classes** that wrap RPCs into strongly-typed generic delegates:

**Client-bound (server sends to client):**
- `ClientInstanceMethod&lt;T1..T12&gt;` -- instance RPCs on NetBehaviours (per-player objects)
- `ClientStaticMethod&lt;T1..T11&gt;` -- static RPCs (global, not tied to a specific object)

**Server-bound (client sends to server):**
- `ServerInstanceMethod&lt;T1..T8&gt;` -- instance RPCs the client sends to server
- `ServerStaticMethod&lt;T1..T8&gt;` -- static RPCs the client sends to server

Each is initialized via a `Get()` factory method during static initialization:

```csharp
// Client-bound instance RPC (server -&gt; specific client)
private static readonly ClientInstanceMethod&lt;EDeathCause, ELimb, CSteamID&gt; SendDeath =
    ClientInstanceMethod&lt;EDeathCause, ELimb, CSteamID&gt;.Get(typeof(PlayerLife), "ReceiveDeath");

// Server-bound instance RPC (client -&gt; server)
private static readonly ServerInstanceMethod&lt;byte, byte, byte&gt; SendEquipRequest =
    ServerInstanceMethod&lt;byte, byte, byte&gt;.Get(typeof(PlayerEquipment), "ReceiveEquipRequest");

// Client-bound static RPC
private static readonly ClientStaticMethod&lt;ushort&gt; SendEffectClearById =
    ClientStaticMethod&lt;ushort&gt;.Get(ReceiveEffectClearById);
```

Sources: [PlayerLife.cs](https://github.com/Unturned-Datamining/Unturned-Datamining/blob/linux-client-preview/Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/PlayerLife.cs), [PlayerEquipment.cs](https://github.com/Unturned-Datamining/Unturned-Datamining/blob/linux-client-preview/Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/PlayerEquipment.cs), [EffectManager.cs](https://github.com/Unturned-Datamining/Unturned-Datamining/blob/linux-client-preview/Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/EffectManager.cs)

---

### 5. How RPCs Are Invoked

**Server sending to clients:**
```csharp
// Send to a single client (e.g., the owning player)
SendDeath.Invoke(GetNetId(), ENetReliability.Reliable,
    base.channel.GetOwnerTransportConnection(), newCause, newLimb, newKiller);

// Send to all clients + execute locally
SendDead.InvokeAndLoopback(GetNetId(), ENetReliability.Reliable,
    Provider.GatherRemoteClientConnections(), ragdoll, ragdollEffect);

// Static RPC to a single connection
SendEffectClearById.Invoke(ENetReliability.Reliable, transportConnection, id);
```

**Client sending to server:**
```csharp
// Simple invocation (no custom serialization)
SendEquipRequest.Invoke(GetNetId(), ENetReliability.Reliable, page, x, y);

// With custom write callback (for complex serialization)
SendInputs.Invoke(GetNetId(), ENetReliability.Reliable, SendInputs_Write);
```

Key parameters:
- `NetId` -- unique network ID for the target object (replacement for old `SteamChannel.id`)
- `ENetReliability.Reliable` or `.Unreliable` -- transport guarantee
- `ITransportConnection` or `List&lt;ITransportConnection&gt;` -- target connection(s)

---

### 6. How RPCs Are Received

Receive methods are decorated with `[SteamCall]` and named with a `Receive` prefix:

```csharp
// Server -&gt; Client: death notification
[SteamCall(ESteamCallValidation.ONLY_FROM_SERVER, legacyName = "tellDeath")]
public void ReceiveDeath(EDeathCause newCause, ELimb newLimb, CSteamID newKiller)

// Client -&gt; Server: equip request (rate-limited)
[SteamCall(ESteamCallValidation.ONLY_FROM_OWNER, ratelimitHz = 2, legacyName = "askToggleVision")]
public void ReceiveToggleVisionRequest()

// Client -&gt; Server: with ServerInvocationContext for reading custom data
[SteamCall(ESteamCallValidation.ONLY_FROM_OWNER)]
public void ReceiveInputs(in ServerInvocationContext context)

// Server -&gt; Client: with ClientInvocationContext
[SteamCall(ESteamCallValidation.ONLY_FROM_SERVER)]
public void ReceiveItemHotkeySuggeston(in ClientInvocationContext context,
    byte hotkeyIndex, Guid expectedAssetGuid, byte page, byte x, byte y)
```

---

### 7. `ServerInvocationContext` and `ClientInvocationContext`

These readonly structs provide RPC call context to receive methods:

**`ServerInvocationContext`** (used in `ONLY_FROM_OWNER`/`SERVERSIDE` methods):
```csharp
public readonly struct ServerInvocationContext
{
    public enum EOrigin { Remote, Loopback, Obsolete }

    public readonly EOrigin origin;
    public readonly NetPakReader reader;    // For reading custom serialized data
    // Also has: callingPlayer, serverMethodInfo (private)

    public Player GetPlayer();
    public SteamPlayer GetCallingPlayer();
    public ITransportConnection GetTransportConnection();
    public bool IsOwnerOf(SteamChannel channel);
    public void Kick(string reason);
}
```

**`ClientInvocationContext`** (used in `ONLY_FROM_SERVER` methods):
```csharp
public readonly struct ClientInvocationContext
{
    public enum EOrigin { Remote, Loopback, Deferred }

    public readonly EOrigin origin;
    public readonly NetPakReader reader;    // For reading custom serialized data
    // Also has: clientMethodInfo (internal)

    public void ReadParameterFailed(string parameterName);  // Debug logging
}
```

Sources: [ServerInvocationContext.cs](https://github.com/Unturned-Datamining/Unturned-Datamining/blob/linux-client-preview/Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/ServerInvocationContext.cs), [ClientInvocationContext.cs](https://github.com/Unturned-Datamining/Unturned-Datamining/blob/linux-client-preview/Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/ClientInvocationContext.cs)

---

### 8. `NetPakReader` / `NetPakWriter` (Serialization)

These replace the old `SteamPacker`. They handle bit-level serialization:

```csharp
// Writing (client side, custom serialization callback)
private void SendInputs_Write(NetPakWriter writer)
{
    if (clientPendingInput is DrivingPlayerInputPacket)
        writer.WriteBit(value: true);
    else
        writer.WriteBit(value: false);
    clientPendingInput.write(writer);
}

// Reading (server side, in ReceiveInputs)
reader.ReadBit(out var value);
PlayerInputPacket playerInputPacket = (!value)
    ? (PlayerInputPacket)new WalkingPlayerInputPacket()
    : (PlayerInputPacket)new DrivingPlayerInputPacket(base.player.movement.getVehicle());
playerInputPacket.read(base.channel, reader);
```

Source: [PlayerInput.cs](https://github.com/Unturned-Datamining/Unturned-Datamining/blob/linux-client-preview/Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/PlayerInput.cs)

---

### 9. Code Generation System

RPC serialization code is **auto-generated at compile time** in the Unity editor. Generated code lives in `Assets/Runtime/Assembly-CSharp/NetGen` (excluded from git). The generated classes use two attributes:

```csharp
// Marks a generated companion class for a specific game type
[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Class, AllowMultiple = false)]
public class NetInvokableGeneratedClassAttribute : Attribute
{
    public readonly Type targetType;
    public NetInvokableGeneratedClassAttribute(Type targetType) { this.targetType = targetType; }
}

// Marks a generated Read or Write method
[AttributeUsage(AttributeTargets.Method, AllowMultiple = false)]
public class NetInvokableGeneratedMethodAttribute : Attribute
{
    public readonly string targetMethodName;
    public readonly ENetInvokableGeneratedMethodPurpose purpose; // Read or Write
}
```

The flow is:
```
SendRPC() -&gt; generatedWrite(NetPakWriter, args)
  -&gt; [over network] -&gt;
generatedRead(NetPakReader) -&gt; ReceiveRPC(deserialized args)
```

Source: [Discussion #4176](https://github.com/SmartlyDressedGames/Unturned-3.x-Community/discussions/4176)

---

### 10. `NetReflection` -- Method Registration

`NetReflection` discovers and registers all RPCs at startup:

```csharp
public static void RegisterFromAssembly(Assembly assembly)
```

This method:
1. Scans all types for `[NetInvokableGeneratedClass]`
2. Collects static methods with `[NetInvokableGeneratedMethod]` (Read/Write pairs)
3. Processes target type methods decorated with `[SteamCall]`
4. Creates delegates for method invocation
5. Populates `clientMethods` and `serverMethods` lists
6. Each method gets a `methodIndex` used in the wire protocol

Key internal state:
```csharp
internal static List&lt;ClientMethodInfo&gt; clientMethods;
internal static uint clientMethodsLength;
internal static int clientMethodsBitCount;    // Bits needed to encode index
internal static List&lt;ServerMethodInfo&gt; serverMethods;
internal static uint serverMethodsLength;
internal static int serverMethodsBitCount;
internal static int rateLimitedMethodsCount;
```

For plugins/modules loaded at runtime, `RegisterFromAssembly()` can be called post-init (Nelson added this specifically for mod support).

Source: [NetReflection.cs](https://github.com/Unturned-Datamining/Unturned-Datamining/blob/linux-client-preview/Assembly-CSharp/SDG.Unturned/NetReflection.cs)

---

### 11. `NetId` -- Network Object Identification

`NetId` replaced `SteamChannel.id`. It is a wrapper around a `uint32`:

```csharp
public struct NetId : IEquatable&lt;NetId&gt;
{
    public static readonly NetId INVALID = new NetId(0u);
    public uint id;
    // ... equality, arithmetic operators, ToString as hex
}
```

Any class with RPCs gets a unique `NetId` when spawned. Instance RPCs use `GetNetId()` to specify which object the RPC targets.

---

### 12. `ENetInvocationDeferMode`

Controls behavior when an instance RPC arrives but the target object does not exist yet (e.g., async loading):

```csharp
public enum ENetInvocationDeferMode
{
    Discard,  // Ignore if target doesn't exist (default for static &amp; server methods)
    Queue     // Buffer invocations for later replay when target spawns
}
```

---

### 13. Old System (Legacy / SteamChannel)

The old system used `SteamChannel` with string-based method lookup and `SteamPacker` for serialization:

```csharp
// Old send pattern (deprecated)
public void send(string name, CSteamID steamID, ESteamPacket type, params object[] arguments)
```

The old `SteamChannel.receive()` method:
1. Extracted a call index from the packet
2. Validated via `ESteamCallValidation`
3. Applied rate limiting
4. Invoked via `MethodInfo.Invoke` (slow, garbage-generating)

`buildCallArray()` scanned components for `[SteamCall]` methods and built a `SteamChannelMethod[]` array capped at 235 methods per channel.

Problems with the old system (from Nelson's blog):
- Argument types determined by reflection, serialized in a huge if/else chain
- Static methods used instance lookup overhead
- Method indices were per-game-object and rebuilt when components added/removed
- Indices could not be initialized until `Start()`
- Indices were not constant, requiring name lookup for rate limiting
- SteamID sender parameters were cross-platform incompatible
- Deserialization via reflection generated garbage
- `MethodInfo.Invoke` was slow

---

### 14. Transport Layer

The `NetTransport` rewrite abstracted `SteamNetworking` into pluggable transport layers:
- Default: `SteamNetworking`
- Alternatives: `-NetTransport=SystemSockets` (Berkeley TCP), `-NetTransport=SteamNetworkingSockets`
- Connections identified by opaque `ITransportConnection` type
- RPC channel encoded as a byte in the RPC header (not per-message)

---

### 15. Complete RPC Flow Example

Using `PlayerEquipment` as a concrete example:

**Client wants to equip an item:**
```csharp
// 1. Client-side: static handle declared once
private static readonly ServerInstanceMethod&lt;byte, byte, byte&gt; SendEquipRequest =
    ServerInstanceMethod&lt;byte, byte, byte&gt;.Get(typeof(PlayerEquipment), "ReceiveEquipRequest");

// 2. Client invokes: serializes (page, x, y) via generated Write delegate
SendEquipRequest.Invoke(GetNetId(), ENetReliability.Reliable, page, x, y);

// 3. Server receives: validated as ONLY_FROM_OWNER, rate limited
[SteamCall(ESteamCallValidation.ONLY_FROM_OWNER, ratelimitHz = 2)]
public void ReceiveEquipRequest(byte page, byte x, byte y) { /* server logic */ }

// 4. Server tells all clients to show the equip
private static readonly ClientInstanceMethod&lt;byte, byte, byte, Guid, byte, byte[], NetId&gt; SendEquip =
    ClientInstanceMethod&lt;byte, byte, byte, Guid, byte, byte[], NetId&gt;
    .Get(typeof(PlayerEquipment), "ReceiveEquip");

SendEquip.InvokeAndLoopback(GetNetId(), ENetReliability.Reliable,
    Provider.GatherRemoteClientConnections(), page, x, y, asset.GUID, quality, state, skinNetId);

// 5. Client receives
[SteamCall(ESteamCallValidation.ONLY_FROM_SERVER)]
public void ReceiveEquip(byte page, byte x, byte y, Guid newAssetGuid,
    byte newQuality, byte[] newState, NetId useableNetId) { /* client render logic */ }
```

---

## Unturned Server-Side Anti-Cheat and Exploit Patches -- Comprehensive Findings

### 1. Server Authority Model

Unturned uses a **hybrid authority model** -- partially server-authoritative, partially client-authoritative, with the balance shifting toward server authority over time through successive patches.

**Client-authoritative elements (still):**
- **Recoil and accuracy** remain client-side. Multiple community sources confirm recoil is client-side data that is "easily modifiable," and moving it server-side would "cause possible lag on servers" and problems for "players with already abnormally high ping." (Source: [Steam Community discussion](https://steamcommunity.com/app/304930/discussions/0/351660338705888040/?l=tchinese&amp;ctp=2))
- **Vehicle movement** is still client-authoritative. The `-ForceTrustClient` launch option "disables movement validation (e.g., position difference between ticks matches speed) for vehicles." Official docs note this flag "should eventually be removed when(/if) vehicle movement is made server authoritative." (Source: [Official launch options docs](https://docs.smartlydressedgames.com/en/stable/about/launch-options.html))

**Server-authoritative elements:**
- **Player movement** is now server-authoritative with client-side prediction (since v3.21.31.0). The server maintains the authoritative position, and when client/server disagree, the client re-simulates unacknowledged inputs.
- **Hit registration / line-of-sight** is server-validated. The server performs line-of-sight checks on bullet hits.
- **Asset/file integrity** is server-validated via hash comparison.
- **Economy info** is server-validated via `Validate_EconInfo_Hash`.

---

### 2. Major Server-Side Exploit Patches (Chronological)

#### v3.20.5.0 (May 2020) -- RPC &amp; Underground Exploits
- **RPC exploit with trees fixed**: "Fixed an undiscovered potential RPC exploit with trees."
- **RPC rate limits individualized**: "All RPCs have individually tuned rate limits to reduce abuse of macro spam. Previously most RPCs shared a common limit."
- **Inventory RPC duplication**: "Fixed sending inventory RPCs twice in some cases."
- **Server-side item stacking**: "Replaced client-side Steam inventory quantity consolidation with automatic server-side stacking."
- **Underground exploit patched**: "Valid underground spaces have been manually designated, so players out-of-bounds are automatically teleported back into the map. Along with fixes to miscellaneous physics exploits this ought to put a stop to underground glitch bases."
- **Physics exploit colliders**: "Converted industrial Dock #2 from mesh collider to box colliders to fix overlap exploits."
- **Map escape exploit**: "Fixed stance transition and obstruction tests to include player clipping. This was abuseable in some places to escape the map border."
- **Asset hash enforcement**: "Master bundle hashes are included in game asset hashes to prevent overriding with modified versions."
(Source: [SteamDB patch notes](https://steamdb.info/patchnotes/5034386/))

#### v3.21.24.0 (Aug 2021) -- Vehicle Physics Validation
- **Server-side vehicle physics validation**: "Verify integrity of client vehicle physics profile on server."
- **RPC deferral**: "Support for deferring RPCs when target object does not exist yet" (prevents race-condition exploits).
(Source: [Steam news](https://store.steampowered.com/news/posts/?feed=steam_community_announcements&amp;appids=304930&amp;appgroupname=Unturned&amp;enddate=1641574540))

#### v3.21.31.0 (Nov 2021) -- Movement Overhaul (Anti-Noclip/Fly)
This was the **single biggest server-side anti-cheat patch**, rewriting the movement system entirely:
- **Server-authoritative movement with client-side prediction**: Previously "when the server position did not match the client position, the client would get teleported back to the server position to prevent cheating" (rubber-banding). Many servers turned this off, "which then allowed cheaters to noclip and fly." The new system lets clients "locally re-simulate inputs that have not been acknowledged by the server yet, sort of rewinding and replaying time, which eliminates the rubberbanding" while keeping server authority.
- **Velocity-based physics**: "Replaced special cases for falling and sliding with actual velocity. This restricts the annoying midair dodging in gunfights."
- **GSLT requirement**: "Setting a Steam game server login token (GSLT) is now required for listings to be visible on the internet server list" to prevent spoofed/fake servers.
- **Legacy networking removed**: v3.21.30.0 "Removed option for servers to use old/legacy/deprecated Steam networking."
(Source: [UpdateCrazy patch notes](https://updatecrazy.com/unturned-update-3-21-31-0-patch-notes-november-19-2021/))

#### v3.21.32.0 (Nov 2021) -- Speed Exploit
- **Slope speed exploit fixed**: "Sliding down slope against wall to build up speed" exploit patched.

#### v3.22.12.0 -- Barricade Netcode
- **Barricade placement on vehicles** updated to use netids rather than index (prevents index-manipulation exploits).
- **Structure stacking exploits**: "Many exploits for stacking housing items in the same slot were fixed."
(Source: [unturned-servers.net](https://unturned-servers.net/blog/378/322120-update-notes/))

#### May 2022 -- HWID Bans &amp; RPC Hardening
- **HWID ban command**: "The vanilla ban command will also ban player HWID."
- **Once-per-connection RPC exploit patched**: "There were 'once per connection' RPCs where the client would request information after it could process it. These were a target for cheaters a few years ago because they would reply with a ton of data, and were overall pointless considering the server should be able to send the data regardless. The request RPCs have been entirely removed now."
- **Network rule for once-per-player requests**: "There was a request which should only be made once per player (which they were sending repeatedly) whose validation was faulty, and rather than fixing just that, a new network rule which was applied to all once-per-player requests was added to prevent the exploit from being found elsewhere."
(Source: [SDG Blog](https://blog.smartlydressedgames.com/))

#### Nov 2022 -- Lag Switch Detection
- **BattlEye lag switch bans**: Starting Nov 3, 2022, temporary bans issued for lag switch usage. As of Nov 10, 2022, **permanent bans** issued.
(Source: [Unturned BattlEye Wiki](https://unturned.wiki.gg/wiki/BattlEye))

#### v3.25.1.0 (2025) -- Line-of-Sight Hardening
- **Removed per-frame limit on server-side LOS checks**: "Removed the once-per-input-frame limit on server-side line-of-sight checks to better protect against bullet hit spoofing at the cost of higher server CPU usage."
- **Vehicle hit validation leniency**: "Server hit validation leniency was increased against players in vehicles to reduce false negatives when shooting fighter jet pilots."
(Source: [unturned-servers.net](https://unturned-servers.net/blog/426/screenshot-contest-2025/))

#### v3.25.7.0 (July 2025) -- ESP Countermeasures &amp; Hit Validation
- **Reduced distant player information**: "Reduced available information about distant players" -- directly makes ESP/radar cheats less useful by not sending data the client doesn't need.
- **Hit validation threshold tightened**: "Significantly tightened server hit validation threshold, making the line-of-sight tests more effective."
- **Barricade/structure instantiation combined**: "Barricade and structure instantiation queues were combined to prevent seeing into loading bases" (anti-ESP during base loading).
(Source: [unturned-servers.net](https://unturned-servers.net/blog/438/32570-update-notes/))

#### v3.25.8.0 -- Barricade Hit Validation
- **Barricade hit bounds tightened**: "Server-side barricade hit validation bounds check was tightened."
(Source: [unturned-servers.net](https://unturned-servers.net/blog/441/32580-update-notes/))

#### v3.26.3.2 -- Building Zone Bypass
- **No-building zone bypass fixed**: "Bypass_No_Building_Zones ignored by several redundant barricade-related checks."
(Source: [PatchBot](https://patchbot.io/games/unturned))

---

### 3. Built-In Server Configuration Anti-Cheat Options

The server's `Config.json` exposes these security knobs (from the [official gameplay config wiki](https://unturned.wiki.gg/wiki/Gameplay_config) and [Restore Monarchy docs](https://restoremonarchy.com/docs/servers/config_json/server)):

| Option | Default | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `VAC_Secure` | `true` | Requires VAC on client |
| `BattlEye_Secure` | `true` | Requires BattlEye on client |
| `Validate_EconInfo_Hash` | `true` | Prevents clients from locally exploiting other players' visibility |
| `Validate_MasterBundle_Hashes` | `true` | Prevents modified/outdated game files from connecting |
| `Rate_Limit_Kick_Threshold` | `10` | Kicks clients hitting same RPC rate limit N times in cooldown window |
| `Enable_Kick_Input_Spam` | `false` | Kicks players spamming `askInput` calls |
| `Enable_Kick_Input_Timeout` | `false` | Kicks players not submitting inputs for extended time |
| `Fake_Lag_Threshold_Seconds` | `3.0` | Flags clients with input packet gaps exceeding threshold |
| `Fake_Lag_Damage_Penalty_Multiplier` | `0.1` | Reduces PvP damage to 10% during detected lag penalties |
| `Max_Packets_Per_Second` | `50.0` | Prevents packet flooding |
| `Max_Ping_Milliseconds` | `750` | Kicks high-latency players |
| `Use_FakeIP` | `false` | Routes through Steam relay to hide server IP from DDoS |

Launch options:
- `-ForceTrustClient`: Disables vehicle movement validation (not recommended)
- `-LogBadMessages`: Logs when server ignores a network message (for diagnosing exploits)
- `-ValidateAssets`: Enhanced asset integrity checks at startup

---

### 4. Third-Party Server-Side Anti-Cheat Plugins

**Darkness-AntiCheat** (RocketMod, abandoned): Detected aimbot via KDA tracking, noclip, anti-aim (impossible rotation angles), fake-lag (Clumsy detection), free-cam abuse, punch/melee distance overrides. (Source: [GitHub](https://github.com/Darkness-Anti-Cheat/Darkness-AntiCheat))

**UnturnedGuard**: Supports RocketMod/OpenMod/Vanilla, includes anti-far-damage, anti-clumsy, micro-lag detection. (Source: [UnturnedGuard](https://unturnedguard.github.io/))

---

### 5. What the Server Still Does NOT Validate

Based on all sources:
- **Recoil/spread**: Fully client-side, easily zeroed by cheats
- **Vehicle movement**: Still client-authoritative (server validation exists but can be disabled with `-ForceTrustClient`)
- **ESP data**: Improved in v3.25.7.0 (reduced distant player info) but the server still sends nearby player positions that ESP can read from memory
- **Aimbot**: Not directly detectable server-side beyond line-of-sight checks and hit-rate heuristics; BattlEye handles this via signature/behavioral detection client-side

---

### 6. Hash Verification System

The server validates client file integrity at connection time:
- **Master bundle hashes** (`Validate_MasterBundle_Hashes`): Server computes hashes of all loaded master bundles; clients with mismatched hashes are rejected. Error: "Client hash is [hash], whereas server hash is [hash]."
- **Level/map hashes**: Logged via `-LogLevelHash`; mismatch produces "This server is using a modified version of the map."
- **Economy info hash** (`Validate_EconInfo_Hash`): Prevents local manipulation of economy/item visibility data.
- **Asset hashes**: Since v3.20.5.0, "master bundle hashes are included in game asset hashes to prevent overriding with modified versions."</result>

<result>Now I have comprehensive data. Here is the full compiled report of all findings:

---

## Unturned Server-Side Validation and Exploits: Comprehensive Research Findings

### 1. NETWORKING ARCHITECTURE

**Unturned 3.x Framework:**
- Uses Unity's built-in networking (UNET/HLAPI). In UNET's default model, the server has authority over non-player GameObjects (items, platforms, NPCs), while player GameObjects have authority on their owner's client. This is the root of many validation gaps.
- No NAT punchthrough support -- servers require port forwarding or public IPs via SteamCMD.
- The game transitioned from a third-party networking framework in 2.x (possibly with NAT punchthrough) to UNET in 3.x.

**Client-Server Trust Model:**
- The core architecture is a **hybrid trust model**: the client computes physics, weapon mechanics, and movement locally, then reports results to the server. The server performs varying degrees of validation depending on the subsystem.
- Key quote from community discussion: "hacks that just modifies the game files, changing GUIs and unity files, sending some fake infos to the server" -- the server accepts many client-reported values.
- Recoil is explicitly **CLIENT SIDE data**, making no-recoil trivially achievable by modifying local values.
- Bullet spread is client-side; "No Spread ensures your bullets go exactly where your crosshair is pointing."
- The `Can_Trust_Client_Movement` config option (Config.json) directly controls whether the server validates movement. When **false** (default on ~99% of servers), server-side movement verification is active. When **true**, it disables verification, enabling noclip/flyhack/speedhack.

Sources:
- [Steam Discussion: How does Unturned handle networking?](https://steamcommunity.com/app/304930/discussions/0/1456202492177827191/)
- [Steam Discussion: Hacker Around some server](https://steamcommunity.com/app/304930/discussions/0/351660338707083414/)
- [Steam Discussion: Unturned hacking - The solution?](https://steamcommunity.com/app/304930/discussions/0/351660338705888040/)
- [Unity Manual: Network Authority](https://docs.unity3d.com/2020.1/Documentation/Manual/UNetAuthority.html)

---

### 2. SERVER-SIDE HIT VALIDATION (Progressive Tightening)

This is the area where SDG (Smartly Dressed Games) has invested the most server-side hardening over time:

**Version 3.22.7.0** -- Introduced anti-lag-switching:
- Added `Fake_Lag_Threshold_Seconds` (default 3s): if delay between input packets exceeds threshold, player gets penalized.
- `Fake_Lag_Damage_Penalty_Multiplier` (default 0.1): during fake lag penalty, damage dealt to other players is reduced to 10%.
- `Fake_Lag_Log_Warnings`: optional server logging of lag events.

**Version 3.25.1.0** -- Major hit validation overhaul:
- **Removed** the once-per-input-frame limit on server-side line-of-sight checks. Previously the server only ran one LoS check per input frame; now it checks every bullet. This "better protects against bullet hit spoofing at the cost of higher server CPU usage."
- Hit types that deal damage now **wait for server confirmation** before playing effects (e.g., shooting a player triggers the blood indicator client-side, but blood splatters only appear after server confirms the hit).
- Increased server hit validation leniency for players in vehicles to reduce false negatives when shooting fighter jet pilots.

**Version 3.25.7.0** -- Tightened thresholds:
- "Significantly tightened server hit validation threshold, making the line-of-sight tests more effective."

**Version 3.25.8.0** -- Bug fixes:
- Fixed bug where "server-side bullet line-of-sight test was using too-recent origin point."
- "Tightened server-side barricade hit validation bounds check."

**Issue #4404** (GitHub) -- An overshoot in validation:
- After these tightening passes, a bug was reported where bullets **fail to register** on entities when the player lacks line-of-sight to the impact location with ballistics enabled. Slow projectiles (50 m/s test) that arc or travel past obstacles get rejected by the LoS check even though they should validly hit. This reveals the tension between anti-cheat strictness and gameplay correctness.

Sources:
- [3.25.7.0 Update Notes](https://unturned-servers.net/blog/438/32570-update-notes/)
- [3.25.8.0 Update Notes](https://unturned-servers.net/blog/441/32580-update-notes/)
- [GitHub Issue #4404: Line of sight check oversight with bullet registration](https://github.com/SmartlyDressedGames/Unturned-3.x-Community/issues/4404)
- [Screenshot Contest 2025 &amp; 3.25.1.0 Update Notes](https://unturned-servers.net/blog/426/screenshot-contest-2025/)

---

### 3. ANTI-CHEAT LAYERS

Unturned uses a **three-layer** anti-cheat approach:

**Layer 1: VAC (Valve Anti-Cheat)**
- Toggled via `VAC_Secure` (default true).
- Signature-based detection of known cheat binaries.

**Layer 2: BattlEye**
- Toggled via `BattlEye_Secure` (default true).
- Scans the OS for hack processes/signatures, similar to antivirus.
- BattlEye's Unturned-specific code is **private** -- even SDG doesn't have access to it.
- Starting November 3, 2022: temporary bans for "lag switch" cheats. Escalated to permanent bans by November 10, 2022.
- A **2025 academic paper** ("Battling The Eye: Exploring the Anti-Cheat Techniques of BattlEye," ACM MATE Workshop 2025) used Unturned as a test case and found design weaknesses allowing unsigned drivers to run despite BattlEye. The study found BattlEye did not detect cheating activity maintained in the same session for several minutes.

**Layer 3: Game-Native Server Validation**
- Asset integrity verification (`Validate_MasterBundle_Hashes`, `Validate_EconInfo_Hash`).
- Server-side hit validation (LoS checks as described above).
- Movement verification (`Can_Trust_Client_Movement`).
- Rate limiting (`Max_Packets_Per_Second` default 50, `Rate_Limit_Kick_Threshold` default 10).
- Input spam/timeout detection (disabled by default: `Enable_Kick_Input_Spam`, `Enable_Kick_Input_Timeout`).
- Fake lag detection and damage penalty system.

Sources:
- [BattlEye - Official Unturned Wiki](https://unturned.wiki.gg/wiki/BattlEye)
- [BattlEye FAQ Steam Discussion](https://steamcommunity.com/app/304930/discussions/0/217691032433994895)
- [ACM Paper: Battling The Eye](https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3733817.3762701)
- [Anti-Cheat FAQ: Bans &amp; Appeals](https://steamcommunity.com/app/304930/discussions/0/805722062315396073/)
- [Gameplay Config - Official Unturned Wiki](https://unturned.wiki.gg/wiki/Gameplay_config)

---

### 4. KNOWN EXPLOITS AND PATCHED VULNERABILITIES

**Item Duplication (GitHub Issue #1303, patched):**
- Exploited `Allow_Per_Character_Saves` setting. When switching characters, items stacked on top of each other in inventory. Players could drop items, relog, and drop identical items again.
- Root cause: server failed to validate inventory state synchronization across character switches. No persistent state verification, missing inventory delta tracking between sessions, potential race conditions in save/load.
- Reveals that inventory validation relied too heavily on client-side state management.

**Vehicle Gravity Hack (GitHub Issue #2697):**
- Cheaters set `useGravity = false` on vehicle rigidbodies, allowing cars to fly.
- No native server-side integrity check for physics properties on vehicles.
- Community had to rely on third-party anti-cheat plugins as workarounds.

**Mod-Based Server Exploits (GitHub Issue #1734):**
- Nelson blocked certain mod capabilities (chat messaging, command execution via `Allow_Client_Messages`/`Allow_Client_Commands` set to false by default).
- However, the reporter demonstrated mods could still trigger `/shutdown` commands to crash servers, kill all players, and make servers unplayable. The fix was characterized as blocking "less dangerous" vulnerabilities while leaving dangerous ones open.

**.dat File Modification:**
- Players could join servers with modified `.dat` item files using tools that bypass ".dat verification."
- Later patches added `Validate_MasterBundle_Hashes` to prevent clients from connecting with modified master bundles.

**Client-Side Weapon Mechanics:**
- Recoil is computed client-side, making no-recoil trivial.
- Bullet spread is client-side, making no-spread trivial.
- Ballistics (bullet drop/travel time) can be disabled client-side by cheat tools ("No Ballistics removes bullet drop and travel time").

Sources:
- [GitHub Issue #1303: Allow_Per_Character_Saves duping items](https://github.com/SmartlyDressedGames/Unturned-3.x-Community/issues/1303)
- [GitHub Issue #2697: Anti Cheat for servers](https://github.com/SmartlyDressedGames/Unturned-3.x-Community/issues/2697)
- [GitHub Issue #1734: Nelson blocked only less dangerous vulnerabilities](https://github.com/SmartlyDressedGames/U3-SDK/issues/1734)
- [Duplication Glitch - MPGH](https://www.mpgh.net/forum/showthread.php?t=840269)

---

### 5. WHAT THE SERVER VALIDATES vs. WHAT IT DOES NOT

**Server Validates:**
- Hit registration via line-of-sight raycasts (progressively tightened since 3.25.x).
- Player movement (when `Can_Trust_Client_Movement` is false, default).
- Asset/bundle integrity hashes on connection.
- Economy info hashes (prevents cosmetic visibility exploits).
- Packet rate limiting and input spam detection.
- Fake lag detection with damage penalty.
- Quest interactions ("only on the authority side, i.e., the server or singleplayer").
- Text chat events ("only fired on the server").
- Damage events pass through `OnPlayerDamaged` with a `canDamage` ref parameter the server can override.
- Barricade/structure placement authority.
- Item spawning is server-controlled via ItemSpawner/AirdropSpawner hooks.

**Server Does NOT Validate (or Validates Weakly):**
- Weapon recoil (purely client-side).
- Bullet spread (purely client-side).
- Ballistic trajectory (client computes drop/travel).
- Vehicle physics properties (gravity, etc.).
- Player position data sent to all clients (enables ESP/wallhacks -- the server sends positions of all players within range regardless of visibility).
- No runtime integrity checking of game files (relies on connection-time hash checks only).
- Jump height and crouch-walk speed (Unturned 2.x had zero validation; 3.x improved but relies on `Can_Trust_Client_Movement`).
- Inventory state transitions during character switches (led to dupe bugs).

Sources:
- [Mod Hooks Documentation](https://docs.smartlydressedgames.com/en/latest/assets/mod-hooks.html)
- [Rocket API Unturned Events](https://restoremonarchy.com/developers/docs/rocket-api/unturnedevents)
- [Gameplay Config - Official Unturned Wiki](https://unturned.wiki.gg/wiki/Gameplay_config)
- [Steam Discussion: Help for Server Owners (Finding Hackers)](https://steamcommunity.com/app/304930/discussions/0/619573787405009372/)

---

### 6. THIRD-PARTY SERVER-SIDE ANTI-CHEAT PLUGINS

The gaps in native validation spawned a community plugin ecosystem:

**Darkness-AntiCheat** (Rocketmod plugin, abandoned):
- Aimbot detection via silent aim analysis and KDA statistics.
- Melee/punch exploit detection (flags override of punch distance).
- Noclip/freecam detection via position monitoring.
- Fake-lag/Clumsy detection via latency pattern analysis.
- Admin abuse monitoring.
- Wall-penetration item collection detection was listed but **never implemented**.
- The existence of this plugin confirms native Unturned lacks: client-side aim verification, movement speed/position validation, hit detection reconciliation, collision state verification, and network behavior anomaly detection.

**UnturnedGuard:**
- Commercial anti-cheat service; documentation is archived. Limited public technical details.

Sources:
- [Darkness-AntiCheat GitHub](https://github.com/Darkness-Anti-Cheat/Darkness-AntiCheat)
- [UnturnedGuard GitHub](https://github.com/UnturnedGuard)
- [ShimmysAdminTools GitHub](https://github.com/ShimmyMySherbet/ShimmysAdminTools)

### 8. CHEAT INJECTION METHODS

**DLL Injection via dnSpy (.NET modification):**
- DeftHack used dnSpy to modify `System.Core.dll`, injecting method calls into the `.cctor` (static constructor), then placing modified DLLs in `Unturned_Data/Managed/`.
- This method explicitly **only works on non-BattlEye, non-VAC servers**.

**External Memory-Based Cheats:**
- Commercial cheat providers advertise BattlEye bypass capabilities with external tools.
- Features commonly offered: ESP (player/item/vehicle), aimbot, no recoil, no spread, no ballistics, wallhack.

Source: [DeftHack GitHub](https://github.com/Letomaniy/DeftHack-Unturned-OFFICIAL)

---

### 9. UNTURNED II DESIGN DIRECTION

- Nelson (SDGNelson) has published devlogs for Unturned II indicating a move toward more server-authoritative architecture, but the specific devlog content (particularly #017, #027) was not accessible (404/redirect errors on the blog).
- The SDG community GitHub issue tracker (#3794) shows requests for server-private anti-cheat systems, but no official developer response on what enhanced server-side validation will look like.
- The move from UNET (deprecated by Unity) to newer networking is implicit in the Unturned II transition.

Source: [Unturned II Dev Tracker](https://devtrackers.gg/unturned/p/e98ec605-official-sdg-what-is-unturned-ii)</result>

<result>Now I have comprehensive data. Let me compile the research findings.

Here is the complete technical research on Arena Breakout: Infinite (ABI) cheat implementations.

---

## 1. Game Executable and Anti-Cheat

- **Process name**: `UAGame.exe` (not "ArenaBreakout" -- this is the actual UE5 binary name)
- **Anti-cheat**: ACE (Anti-Cheat Expert) by Tencent -- kernel-level driver, pattern recognition, heuristic analysis, DMA device scanning ("DMA Hunter")
- **Engine**: Unreal Engine 5 (modified/custom build)

## 2. Global Offsets (from SuckaSkidding/Arena-breakout-infinite-cheat)

The one real open-source ABI cheat found is at `github.com/SuckaSkidding/Arena-breakout-infinite-cheat`. These offsets are version-specific and will shift on game updates:

```cpp
OFFSET_GWORLD           = 0xAB9F1A8
OFFSET_GOBJECTS         = 0xB031548
OFFSET_GNAMES           = 0xB017140
OFFSET_FNAME_ENCRYPTION_KEY = 0xB016D88
```

A Dumpspace entry exists for ABI at hash `05b736da` (`dumpspace.spuckwaffel.com/Games/?hash=05b736da`) with Dumper-7 SDK dumps, but the site requires JS rendering to access class data.

## 3. FName Encryption -- ABI DOES encrypt FNames

ABI uses XOR-based FName encryption. There are two decryption routines found, suggesting the scheme changed between versions:

**Variant 1** (primary):
```cpp
void FNameDecrypt(char* inputBuf, int namelength) {
    uint8_t xor_key = Read&lt;uint8_t&gt;(BaseAddress + 0xB016D88);
    uint8_t v4 = xor_key ^ ((xor_key &gt;&gt; 5) &amp; 2) ^ (32 * ((xor_key ^ ((xor_key &gt;&gt; 5) &amp; 2)) &amp; 2));
    for (int i = 0; i &lt; namelength; ++i)
        inputBuf[i] = v4 ^ inputBuf[i] ^ ((v4 &gt;&gt; 5) &amp; 2) ^ 0x4D;
}
```

**Variant 2** (alternate key at different address):
```cpp
void FFNameDecrypt(char* inputBuf, int namelength) {
    char xor_key = Read&lt;BYTE&gt;(BaseAddress + 0xB011C08);
    char a2 = ((unsigned __int8)xor_key &gt;&gt; 5) &amp; 2;
    // ... same structure but XOR constant is 0x55 instead of 0x4D
}
```

Key detail: the XOR key is read from a global in the binary at runtime, not hardcoded. The decryption XOR constants (`0x4D` / `0x55`) differ between the two variants.

**FName resolution** (GNames / FNamePool):
```cpp
std::string GetNameFromFName(int key) {
    auto chunkOffset = (UINT)((int)(key) &gt;&gt; 16);
    auto nameOffset  = (USHORT)key;
    auto namePoolChunk = Read&lt;uintptr_t&gt;(BaseAddress + OFFSET_GNAMES + ((chunkOffset + 2) * 8));
    auto entryOffset   = namePoolChunk + (ULONG)(2 * nameOffset);
    auto nameEntry     = Read&lt;INT16&gt;(entryOffset);
    auto nameLength    = nameEntry &gt;&gt; 6;
    // read nameLength bytes at entryOffset+2, then FNameDecrypt
}
```

## 4. Entity Traversal Chain

Standard UE5 chain with one notable difference -- `ActorArray` is at offset `0x98` in ULevel, not the standard `0xd8`:

```
GWorld (0xAB9F1A8)
  -&gt; UWorld::PersistentLevel (0x30)
      -&gt; ULevel::ActorArray (0x98)     // &lt;-- nonstandard, was 0xd8
  -&gt; UWorld::OwningGameInstance (0x180)
      -&gt; UGameInstance::LocalPlayers (0x38)
          -&gt; UPlayer::PlayerController (0x30)
  -&gt; UWorld::GameState (0x120)
      -&gt; AGameStateBase::PlayerArray (0x328)
```

Actor identification uses the FName ID at pawn+24:
```cpp
uint32_t object_id = Read&lt;uint32_t&gt;(pawn + 24);
std::string name = GetNameFromFName(object_id);
```

**Player class names**: `BP_UamCharacter`, `BP_UamAICharacter_C`, `BP_UamRangeCharacter_C`

**Loot class names**: strings containing `nade`, `GrenadeBox_C`, `SportBag_C`, `WeaponCase_C`, `ToolBox_C`, `MunitionBox_C`, `Inventory_`, `LootBoxBase_`

## 5. Character Type Enum

ABI has a custom character type system at offset `0x152D` from the pawn (inside `ASGCharacter`):

```cpp
enum class ECharacterType : int8_t {
    None = 0, PMC = 1, SCAV = 2, AI_SCAV = 3,
    AI_SCAV_BOSS = 4, AI_PMC = 5, AI_ELIT = 6,
    BOSS = 7, AI_SCAV_Follower = 8, MAX = 9
};
```

This closely mirrors EFT's PMC/Scav taxonomy.

## 6. Bone Structure

Bone indices for the skeleton (from `CachedBoneArray` accessed via mesh + 0x648):

```cpp
struct Bones {
    Root = 0, Pelvis = 1,
    Spine_01 = 12, Spine_02 = 13, Spine_03 = 14,
    Neck = 15, Head = 16,
    Thigh_L = 2, Calf_L = 4, Foot_L = 5,
    Thigh_R = 7, Calf_R = 9, Foot_R = 10,
    Clavicle_L = 50, UpperArm_L = 51, LowerArm_L = 52, Hand_L = 54,
    Clavicle_R = 20, UpperArm_R = 21, LowerArm_R = 22, Hand_R = 24,
    Item_R = 40, Item_L = 70, Camera = 80
};
```

**Bone position retrieval**:
```cpp
// BoneArray at mesh + 0x648 (fallback: mesh + 0x658)
auto boneArray = Read&lt;uintptr_t&gt;(mesh + 0x648);
if (boneArray == 0) boneArray = Read&lt;uintptr_t&gt;(mesh + 0x648 + 0x10);
f_transform bone = Read&lt;f_transform&gt;(boneArray + index * 0x30);

// ComponentToWorld is a POINTER at mesh + 0x190
auto c2w_ptr = Read&lt;uintptr_t&gt;(mesh + 0x190);
f_transform c2w = Read&lt;f_transform&gt;(c2w_ptr);
// Multiply bone.to_matrix() * c2w.to_matrix() -&gt; world position
```

Important: `ComponentToWorld` at offset `0x190` in `USkeletalMeshComponent` is stored as a **pointer to** the transform, not the transform itself. This is nonstandard.

## 7. Health System

ABI uses a **per-limb health model** via `USGCharacterEnduranceAttributeSet`, very similar to EFT:

```cpp
struct USGCharacterEnduranceAttributeSet {
    float HeadLife, HeadMaxLife;
    float ChestLife, ChestMaxLife;
    float StomachLife, StomachMaxLife;
    float LeftArmLife, LeftArmMaxLife;
    float RightArmLife, RightArmMaxLife;
    float LeftLegLife, LeftLegMaxLife;
    float RightLegLife, RightLegMaxLife;
};
// Access: pawn + 0x15E0 -&gt; AbilitySystemComponent
//         ASC + 0x188 -&gt; SpawnedAttributes
//         SpawnedAttributes + 0x08 -&gt; first attribute set
//         attribute + 0x48 -&gt; USGCharacterEnduranceAttributeSet
```

There is also a simple `Health` float at `ASGCharacter + 0x199C`.

**Death detection**: `ASGCharacter::DeathComponent` at `0x1728`, then check `DeathComponent + 0x240` for death state.

## 8. Team Detection

```cpp
auto MyTeamID = Read&lt;uint32_t&gt;(LocalPlayerState + 0x520);
auto TeamID   = Read&lt;uint32_t&gt;(enemyPlayerState + 0x520);
// Same = friendly
```

Player name: `FString` at `PlayerState + 0x3F0`.

## 9. Key ABI-Specific Offsets (ASGCharacter namespace)

| Offset | Field | Notes |
|--------|-------|-------|
| 0x152D | CachedCharacterType | ECharacterType enum |

## 10. Standard UE5 Offsets Used

| Struct | Field | Offset |
|--------|-------|--------|
| AActor | RootComponent | 0x168 |
| APawn | PlayerState | 0x340 |

## 11. Driver / Memory Read Approach

The SuckaSkidding cheat uses a **custom kernel driver** communicating via IOCTL:
- Device name: generated from a hash seed `0x2F9C6B1F9` (compile-time computed)
- IOCTL codes: `CODE_RW` (read/write), `CODE_BA` (base address), `CODE_GET_DIRBASE` (CR3)
- Communication: `DeviceIoControl` with transfer_data structs containing key, PID, source, dest, length
- Has mouse move IOCTL for aimbot input injection
- The cheat is external, uses NVIDIA overlay hijacking for rendering

## 12. What is NOT present / notable gaps

- **No aimbot implementation found** in open source. The source has mouse_move IOCTL support in the driver but no aimbot logic in the usermode code.
- **No recoil compensation** code found in any public source.
- **No DMA-specific open source** -- all DMA cheats for ABI are commercial/closed. The SuckaSkidding cheat uses a traditional kernel driver, not DMA hardware.
- **Loot container contents reading** is partially implemented (commented-out code in cache.hpp referencing `USGInventoryContainerMgrComponent + 0x140` for `InventoryContainerBaseList` and item prices at `common_data_comp + 0x10C`, names at `+ 0x138`).
- **No pointer encryption on GWorld/GObjects** -- only FNames are encrypted.
- **Player count** read at `GameState + 0x330` (0x328 + 0x8).
- The `frida-ue4dump` tool has an open issue about failing to find `GUObjectArray` on Arena Breakout Android, suggesting the mobile version has additional obfuscation.</result>
